â€¢ ••The importance of believing we are crucified with Christ••
	• Leads to comfort, spiritual life, and a deeper understanding of God's love
	• Essential for experiencing the benefits of Christ's passion

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

••1. Crucifixion with Christ••

• To be crucified with Christ, you must:
	• Apply your heart to crucify the body of corruption in you
	• Use prayer, fasting, and avoiding sin to overcome corruption
	• Abstain from sin and its occasions
	• Live a life of good means and habits

••2. Conformity with Christ's Sufferings••

• To be conformable to Christ's sufferings, you must:
	• Suffer in love, increasing your love for God and man
	• Resign yourself to God's will and quiet yourself in His will
	• Humble yourself under God's mighty hand, confessing your sins
	• Depend on God's goodness, even in the midst of suffering
	• Suffer constantly, even unto death, in resisting sin

••3. The Fruit of Suffering••

• When suffering, your care should be to:
	• See the fruit of your distress, rather than seeking deliverance
	• Trust that God is working in your life, even in suffering

••4. False Faith••

• Many people have a false faith, believing that:
	• Christ was crucified for them, but not that they are crucified with Him
	• Their faith is incomplete and lacks the power of God

••5. Knowing Christ Crucified••

• To truly know Christ crucified, you must:
	• Believe that you are crucified with Him
	• Understand that Christ's crucifixion is not just a historical event, but a present reality in your life

••6. The Abomination of the Church of Rome••

• The Church of Rome abuses the treasure of Christ crucified by:
	• Worshiping Him in painted and carved crucifixes, rather than in the living Christ
	• Giving divine honor to devised and framed crucifixes, rather than to the true Christ

••7. Abnegation••

• To live a spiritual life, you must:
	• Deny your natural desires and impulses
	• Live as a man crucified, carrying yourself as a dead man
	• Suffer nothing of your sinful nature to reign in you

••8. Denying Corruption••

• To deny corruption, you must:
	• Esteem yourself as unworthy of basic needs like food, drink, and sleep
	• Deny ungodliness and worldly lusts, not suffering them to reign over you

••9. Living as a Dead Man••

• To live as a dead man, you must:
	• Deny the good things of this world, not suffering them to reign over you
	• Live a life of self-denial and humility

••10. Spiritual Life••

• To live a spiritual life, you must:
	• Live a life of crucifixion, carrying yourself as a dead man
	• Suffer nothing of your sinful nature to reign in you
	• Let Christ alone live and reign in you.

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

••1. Living for Christ••
	• We must daily sacrifice worldly honors, riches, pleasures, and friends for Christ's sake.
	• We must not allow these things to take root in our hearts.
	• We must be willing to "die" to our own desires and wills.

••2. Dying to our own reason and will••
	• We must submit our reason and will to God's will.
	• We must make God's will our wisdom and will.
	• Our own wills must be "dead" in us, allowing God's will to reign.

••3. Forgetting our old life••
	• We must forget our old life, including our family and friends.
	• We must be willing to "sell all" we have, including our wills and affections.
	• We must be willing to "die" to our old life in order to live for Christ.

••4. The source of spiritual life••
	• Christ is the root of spiritual life, having life in Himself.
	• He is the true vine, and we are the branches.
	• He is the Prince of life, and a quickening spirit.

••5. Union with Christ••
	• We must be united with Christ in order to receive life from Him.
	• We must be grafted onto Him, just as a branch is grafted onto a vine.
	• This union is a substantial union, made by the bond of one Spirit.

••6. The nature of this union••
	• This union is spiritual, not physical.
	• It is made by the bond of one Spirit, through baptism.
	• We are joined to Christ in heaven, just as a man and wife are joined in marriage.

••7. Communication of Christ's life••
	• After union with Christ, He must further communicate Himself unto us.
	• This communication is necessary for us to live by Him and in Him.
	• God has given us life, and this life is in the Son.

••8. The order of Christ's communication••
	• Christ first gives Himself to us, and then His gifts.
	• His gifts include the efficacy and merit of His death.
	• The institution of the Lord's Supper shows that we must first receive Christ Himself.

••9. How Christ lives in us••
	• Christ is not in us in respect of local presence, but by the supernatural operation of His Spirit.
	• The operation of the Spirit is threefold: imputation of righteousness, giving of eternal life, and the earnest of this right.

••10. The operation of the Spirit••
	• The operation of the Spirit is threefold: imputation of righteousness, giving of eternal life, and the earnest of this right.
	• This operation is necessary for us to live by Christ and in Christ.

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

â€¢ ••Justification and the Fruits of the Spirit••
	• Justification is called "the justification of life" (Rom. 5)
	• The first fruits of the Spirit are justification, vivification, and the resurrection of the dead

â€¢ ••Vivification by the Virtue of Christ's Resurrection••
	• Vivification is the power of the Godhead of Christ, raising us to new life
	• This power is the same one that raised Christ from the dead

â€¢ ••The Resurrection of the Dead••
	• The third fruit of the Spirit is the resurrection of the dead body to everlasting glory
	• This will happen on the day of judgment (Rom. 8:11)

â€¢ ••Christ Living in Believers••
	• Christ lives in believers as a root or head
	• This is made possible by the operation of the Spirit, causing them to die to sin and live to God

â€¢ ••The Consequences of Christ Living in Believers••
	• True believers cannot make a practice of sin
	• They sin not with the full consent of their wills, but rather out of ignorance or infirmity

â€¢ ••The Security of Believers••
	• True believers cannot wholly fall away from grace
	• The life of Christ cannot be abolished, and they are made alive to God

â€¢ ••The Freedom of Believers••
	• True believers are a free and voluntary people, obeying God without compulsion
	• They have Christ living in them, and the Spirit of life is their law

â€¢ ••How to Know Christ Lives in Us••
	• We can know Christ lives in us by the Spirit of God (1 John 3:24)
	• The Spirit is known by its motions and operations, including a purpose to obey God

â€¢ ••Signs of the Spirit's Operation••
	• A purpose to obey God according to all His commandments
	• An inclination of the heart to God's laws
	• A mind and disposition like Christ's, seeking God's glory and the good of others

â€¢ ••The Evidence of Christ's Presence••
	• Loving Christ for Himself and loving those who love Christ
	• This is a true sign that we have passed from death to life (1 John 3:14)

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

â€¢ ••The Life of Christ in Us••
	• The life of Christ is made manifest in us through spiritual life
	• Natural life decays, but spiritual life takes its place
	• We bear the mortification of Christ in our bodies to make His life manifest

â€¢ ••The Means of Spiritual Life••
	• God conveys the life of Christ to us in small increments
	• We must be wounded and slain by God before He can revive us
	• The life of Christ in us makes us feel the mass and body of corruption

â€¢ ••The Sin of Rejecting Christ••
	• Men will not suffer Christ to live in them and rule over them
	• This is a grievous offense, as seen in the Gentiles and the Jews
	• Christ says that those who reject Him will be brought before Him to be slain

â€¢ ••Our Duty to Christ••
	• We must seek Christ's kingdom above all things
	• We must take His yoke on us and use the means appointed to live for Him
	• We must spiritually "eat the flesh of Christ and drink His blood"

â€¢ ••The Means of Spiritual Life (continued)••
	• We live by faith in the Son of God, who has loved us and given Himself for us
	• Faith is an instrument to unite us to Christ and receive life from Him
	• Faith is a guide to order and govern our temporal life according to God's will

â€¢ ••How We Live by Faith••
	• We live a double life in this world: spiritual and temporal
	• The spiritual life stands in three things: reconciliation with God, renovation of life, and good works
	• We live in this world only by faith in our reconciliation with God

â€¢ ••The Importance of Faith••
	• Faith is an instrument to unite us to Christ and receive life from Him
	• Faith is a guide to order and govern our temporal life according to God's will
	• Faith persuades us to good duties and governs our lives

â€¢ ••The Spiritual Life••
	• The spiritual life is a life of reconciliation with God
	• We enjoy pardon of our sins, imputation of justice, and acceptance to life eternal only by means of our faith
	• The spiritual life is a life of renovation and good works

â€¢ ••The Means of Spiritual Life (again)••
	• We must use the means appointed to live for Christ, such as meditation, prayer, and sacraments
	• We must spiritually "eat the flesh of Christ and drink His blood"
	• We must have a stomach in our souls like the stomach of our bodies and hunger and thirst after Christ

â€¢ ••The Result of Spiritual Life••
	• Our wretched souls will be quickened and revived to everlasting life
	• We will be able to say with good conscience that "Christ lives in us"
	• We will be able to live a life that is pleasing to God and brings glory to Christ

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

â€¢ ••Living by Faith••
	• We live by faith in Christ, which purifies our hearts and moves us to holiness and newness of life (Acts 15:9, 1 John 3:3)
	• Faith is essential for every good work, including a general faith that the work pleases God (Rom. 14:25)

â€¢ ••Faith in Action••
	• Justifying faith gives a beginning to the work (Ps. 116:10)
	• Faith must cover the defects of the work, making it acceptable to God (Heb. 11:6)

â€¢ ••Living by Faith in Temporal Life••
	• We are to live by faith in our worldly cares, doing our office and labor with diligence (1 Peter 5:7)
	• We must cast our care on God, trusting in His promise for a good issue (1 Cor. 10:13)

â€¢ ••Faith in Afflictions••
	• Faith assures us that God will give a good issue, even in the midst of afflictions (1 Cor. 10:13)
	• Faith helps us wait for God's leisure for our deliverance (Isa. 16:28)

â€¢ ••Faith in Temptations••
	• We are not to live by feeling, but by faith, even against feeling (Ps. 116:10)
	• We must rest on the bare promise of God, even when we feel nothing but wrath (Rom. 8:28)

â€¢ ••The Faith We Live By••
	• The faith we live by is the faith of the Son of God (John 1:12)
	• This faith is revealed to us through Christ, who is the author and object of our faith

â€¢ ••The Nature of Saving Faith••
	• Saving faith is a faith that believes Christ has loved and given Himself for us (Rom. 5:8)
	• This faith is essential for living by faith in Christ

â€¢ ••Objections to Living by Faith••
	• Some object that they live by sense, believing only what they see (Ps. 115:4-5)
	• Others put too much confidence in means, rather than trusting God's bare word

â€¢ ••The Dangers of Not Living by Faith••
	• Those who live only by sense or reason will miss their mark and not attain eternal life (Heb. 11:6)
	• Those who think they can live as they list and call upon God only when dying will not truly die by faith

â€¢ ••The Importance of Living by Faith••
	• We must live by faith, not just die by faith, in order to truly live for God (Rom. 14:25)
	• Those who spend their days in worldly pursuits without living by faith will be blamed for their lack of faith.

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

â€¢ ••The Life of Infidels••
	• Characterized by a multitude of cares and worries
	• Prevents good things from happening
	• Can be contrasted with the life of faith, which brings peace and contentment

â€¢ ••Living by Faith in Perilous Times••
	• Examples of faith in action: Noah, Abraham, Isaac, Jacob, Moses, David, and Jehoshaphat
	• Faith involves trusting in God's invisible presence and power

â€¢ ••The Importance of Faith••
	• Faith is essential for living a life that is better than that of a beast
	• Requires acquainting oneself with God's word and promises
	• Mingles faith with the love of God and the merit of Christ's passion

â€¢ ••The Nature of Justifying Faith••
	• Applies the love of God and the merit of Christ's passion to oneself
	• Not just a feeling or emotion, but a will and endeavor to believe

â€¢ ••Faith and Hope••
	• Hope is not the primary application of faith, but rather faith is the foundation for hope
	• Papists are deceived in thinking that hope, rather than faith, is the key to salvation

â€¢ ••The Mind and Will in Faith••
	• The mind must be fixed on Christ
	• The will must be to believe and apprehend Christ
	• God accepts the true and earnest will to believe for faith

â€¢ ••The Perfection of Faith••
	• Not required for salvation, but rather the perfection of obedience to Christ
	• Faith apprehends the promise of God, which is the basis for healing and salvation

â€¢ ••The Poor in Spirit••
	• Those who find themselves empty of all goodness and full of unbelief
	• Those who unfeignedly desire to believe are accepted by God as believers

â€¢ ••The Reason for Living to God••
	• Christ loves us and has given Himself for us
	• Therefore, we should live to God and obey His commands

â€¢ ••The Hardness of Hearts••
	• A cause for concern and bewailing
	• Prevents us from relenting from evil ways and turning to God
	• Can lead to a life of spiritual barrenness and desolation

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

â€¢ ••Paul's Defense of Justification by Faith••
	• Paul argues that justification by faith alone does not abolish the grace of God.
	• He says that if a sinner is justified by their own obedience to the law, then Christ died in vain.

â€¢ ••The Objection to Justification by Faith••
	• Some people object that teaching justification by faith alone abolishes the grace of God.
	• Paul responds that this is not the case.

â€¢ ••The Nature of Grace••
	• Paul says that God's grace cannot stand with human merit.
	• Grace must be freely given, and it cannot be earned through works.

â€¢ ••The Relationship Between Grace and Works••
	• Paul says that grace and works of grace cannot stand together.
	• This is because grace is freely given, and works are earned through merit.

â€¢ ••The Duty to Ascribe All to God's Grace••
	• We must be careful not to abrogate the grace of God unto ourselves.
	• We must strip ourselves of all righteousness and goodness of our own and ascribe it to God's grace.

â€¢ ••The Death of Christ and Justification••
	• The death of Christ is made void if anything is joined with it in the work of our justification.
	• This means that the doctrine of justification by works is a manifest error.

â€¢ ••The Error of Human Satisfaction••
	• The doctrine of human satisfaction is a device of man's brain.
	• If we satisfy for ourselves, then Christ died in vain to satisfy for us.

â€¢ ••The Error of Meriting by Works••
	• It is a false and wicked idea to say that Christ by His death merited that we should merit by our works.
	• If we merit by works, Christ died in vain to merit by His own death.

â€¢ ••The Error of the Church of Rome••
	• The Church of Rome joins the merit of man's works and the merit of the death of Christ in the justification of a sinner.
	• This is a false and wicked foundation of true religion.

â€¢ ••The Incompatibility of Light and Darkness••
	• Light and darkness cannot be reconciled, nor can fire and water.
	• This means that the Church of Rome's error cannot be reconciled with the truth of justification by faith alone.

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

• ••Paul's Argument••
	• Paul argues that if he was called by God and his doctrine is true, the Galatians should not have revolted to another gospel.
	• He repeats the conclusion of his argument from Galatians 1:6.
• ••Causes of the Galatians' Revolt••
	• Paul identifies two causes: folly and the deceit of false teachers.
	• He calls the Galatians "foolish" because they have overturned the gospel of Christ.
• ••Interpretation of "O Foolish Galatians"••
	• Paul's judgment is not against the Galatians themselves, but against their new doctrine and practice.
	• He gives this judgment by virtue of his calling as an apostle.
• ••Righteousness of Paul's Judgment••
	• Paul's judgment is righteous and true because it is based on good ground.
	• He gives this judgment in love, intending to correct and amend the Galatians.
• ••Similar Examples in Scripture••
	• Isaiah calls the Israelites "people of Sodom and Gomorrah" (Isaiah 1).
	• Christ calls the two disciples "foolish and slow of heart to believe" (Luke 24:25).
• ••Using Judgment Against Men••
	• We may use like judgment against men if we have a warrant and calling from God.
	• Judgment must be in truth, charity, and for the amendment of the parties.
• ••The Nature of Witchcraft••
	• Paul says that the Galatians have been "bewitched" by false teachers.
	• He takes it for granted that there is witchcraft and witches.
• ••Interpretation of "Who Hath Bewitched You?"••
	• The witchcraft here meant is a satanical operation that deludes the senses of men.
	• The devil can corrupt the fantasy or imagination and cause men to think things that are not true.
• ••Examples of Satanical Operations••
	• The devil can delude the outward senses, as in the case of James and Jambres turning their rods into serpents.
	• There is a disease called lycanthropy in which men think they are wolves and carry themselves as wolves.
• ••Conclusion••
	• Paul's argument is that if he was called by God and his doctrine is true, the Galatians should not have revolted to another gospel.
	• He identifies the causes of their revolt and calls them to repentance.

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

â€¢ ••The Devil's Deception••
	• The devil deceives people by making them believe lies
	• He uses counterfeit or fake versions of God's truth to lead people astray
	• Examples include the witch of Endor and the frogs brought by Moses (Ex. 7-8)

â€¢ ••Men's Involvement with the Devil••
	• Men can be involved with the devil through a league or confederacy
	• The devil offers himself to people and provides them with "sacraments" and "words" to follow
	• He requires faith from those who follow him, just like God does

â€¢ ••The Devil's Methods••
	• The devil uses charms, imprecations, curses, and superstitious invocations to achieve his goals
	• He gives a "watch word" to those who follow him, and they are able to do his bidding
	• This is how men are said to bewitch or delude others

â€¢ ••The Galatians' Bewitchment••
	• The Galatians were bewitched by the devil, leading them to follow a false gospel
	• They added justification by works to the doctrine of justification by faith alone
	• This addition showed that they did not truly believe the truth

â€¢ ••The Consequences of Bewitchment••
	• Bewitchment can lead people to follow a false gospel and be deceived by the devil
	• It can cause people to be foolish and besotted, leading them to perdition
	• It is essential to learn the way of life in humility and obey God's word

â€¢ ••The Importance of Loving God's Word••
	• People must love God's word with all their heart, mind, and soul
	• If they do not love God's word, they will be given up to strong delusions and believe lies
	• This is a warning from 2 Thessalonians 2:10-11

â€¢ ••The Need for Guidance••
	• People must pray to God to be taught and guided by His word and Spirit
	• This is essential for understanding things pertaining to everlasting life

â€¢ ••The Corruption of the Bible••
	• Some people may say that the Bible is corrupted because of differences in translations and editions
	• However, the sense of the Scripture remains entire, sound, and incorrupt

â€¢ ••The Obedience of Faith••
	• The obedience of faith is the obedience of faith alone, without adding or changing
	• The Galatians added justification by works to the doctrine of justification by faith alone, which is a perversion of the truth

â€¢ ••The Devil's Goal••
	• The devil's ultimate goal is to hinder or overthrow our faith
	• He aimed to do this with Adam and Eve in the Garden of Eden, and continues to do so today

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

â€¢ ••The Devil's Attack on Faith••
	• The devil tempted Jesus to doubt His faith as the Son of God
	• The devil seeks to sift out faith and leave only unbelief
	• This must teach us to build ourselves on our faith and not boast of its strength

â€¢ ••The Truth of the Gospel••
	• The gospel is called "the truth" because it is an absolute truth without error
	• The apostles and prophets could not err in their preaching and writing because they were inspired by the Holy Ghost

â€¢ ••Judgment and Error••
	• There are two types of judgment: natural reason and revelation
	• The apostles and prophets could err in natural reason, but not in revelation

â€¢ ••Knowing the Truth••
	• To know the truth, we must first make ourselves fit to know it
	• We are fit to know the truth if we first obey it (John 7:17)

â€¢ ••The Delusion of the Galatians••
	• The Galatians were deluded because they could not acknowledge the truth when it was set before them
	• This is a sign of delusion when we cannot recognize the truth when it is clearly presented

â€¢ ••Properties of the Ministry of the Word••
	• The ministry of the word must be plain, perspicuous, and evident
	• The Church of Rome is criticized for keeping the Scriptures in an unknown tongue

â€¢ ••Plain Preaching••
	• Plain preaching is essential for painting Christ before the eyes of men
	• The plainer the preaching, the better it is

â€¢ ••Powerful and Lively Preaching••
	• The ministry of the word must be powerful and lively in operation
	• It should cause us to feel the virtue of Christ's passion and crucify Christ within us

â€¢ ••The Importance of Obedience••
	• Obedience is essential for knowing the truth (John 7:17)
	• We must first obey the truth before we can know it

â€¢ ••The Sign of Delusion••
	• The sign of delusion is when we cannot acknowledge the truth when it is clearly presented
	• This is a warning to us to be careful not to fall into delusion.

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

â€¢ ••The Power of Prophecy••
	• True prophecy judges men and discovers the things of the heart
	• It causes men to say, "The Lord is within you" (1 Cor. 14:25)
	• Prophecy is a two-edged sword that pierces into the heart (Heb. 4:12)

â€¢ ••The Ministry of the Word••
	• The ministry of the word is the scepter of Christ (Isa. 11:4)
	• It shakes heaven and earth (Hag. 2:5-6)
	• It is the means by which Christ preaches and judges men

â€¢ ••Effective Preaching••
	• Effective preaching stands in three things:
		- True and proper interpretation of Scripture
		- Savory and wholesome doctrine gathered from Scripture
		- Application of doctrine to judgment or life

â€¢ ••Images in the Church••
	• Christian images and pictures are necessary in the congregations of God
	• These images are sermons of Christ and the right administration of sacraments
	• Painted and carved images of the papists are idols and should be detested

â€¢ ••The Need for Lively Preaching••
	• Where the lively preaching of the word is, there is no need for images
	• Images were not established in churches in the West until after 700 years

â€¢ ••Painting Christ in Churches••
	• The Lord allows the description of Christ in speech, but not in images
	• Carving or painting images in churches for religious use is condemned (Ex. 20:4-6)

â€¢ ••The Sacrifice of Christ••
	• There is a lively representation of the passion of Christ in preaching and the Lord's Supper
	• This representation is a sacrifice or oblation of Christ, but not in the same way as the Mass

â€¢ ••The Mass as an Abomination••
	• The Mass is an abomination and a mockery
	• The priest becomes a mediator between Christ and God, which is not biblical

â€¢ ••The Duty of Believers••
	• The duty of all believers is to behold Christ crucified
	• This should be done by looking at Christ as He propounds Himself in the word and sacraments

â€¢ ••The Eye of Faith••
	• Believers should behold Christ with the eye of faith, which makes them see and feel Him crucified in them
	• Implicit faith is not enough, but rather a faith that sees and feels Christ crucified in them.

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

â€¢ ••The Importance of Contemplating Christ••
	• The author believes that contemplating Christ is essential for spiritual growth
	• This contemplation is often neglected, and people instead focus on external appearances

â€¢ ••The Evil Eye and Adulterous Eye••
	• The "evil eye" and "adulterous eye" refer to the tendency to focus on external things rather than spiritual truths
	• This can lead to a lack of spiritual depth and understanding

â€¢ ••The Eye of Faith••
	• The "eye of faith" is the ability to behold Christ and understand spiritual truths
	• This eye is essential for spiritual growth and understanding

â€¢ ••The Benefits of Contemplating Christ••
	• Contemplating Christ crucified reveals our own misery and wickedness
	• It brings true and lively comfort, as we see paradise in the midst of hell
	• It makes us mourn and bleed in our hearts for our offenses
	• It makes us love Christ and understand the love of God in Him

â€¢ ••The Universal Change of Contemplating Christ••
	• Contemplating Christ makes a universal change in us, as we take on the disposition and mind of Christ
	• This change is like the chameleon, which takes on the colors of its surroundings

â€¢ ••The Terror of the Ungodly••
	• Those who do not contemplate Christ will be terrified of Him in the day of judgment
	• They will see Christ as their judge, whom they have pierced

â€¢ ••The Importance of Receiving the Spirit••
	• The author asks the Galatians how they received the Spirit
	• He is concerned that they may have received the Spirit through works of the law rather than through faith

â€¢ ••The Folly of Seeking Perfection through the Flesh••
	• The author asks the Galatians if they are foolish to seek perfection through the flesh after beginning in the Spirit
	• He is concerned that they may be trying to achieve spiritual growth through external means rather than through faith

â€¢ ••The Meaning of Receiving the Spirit••
	• The Spirit can refer to the essential Spirit of the Father and the Son, or to the effects and operations of the Spirit
	• In this text, the Spirit refers to the Spirit of adoption
	• To receive the Spirit means to have the Spirit present in us and to have the Spirit testify to His presence through special operations

â€¢ ••The Two Aspects of Receiving the Spirit••
	• Receiving the Spirit involves two things: the presence of the Spirit in us and the Spirit's special operations
	• The Spirit's presence is not just a matter of receiving gifts, but also involves the Spirit's active work in our lives

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

â€¢ ••The Spirit and Gifts of Grace••
	• The Spirit is a person, not just a gift or effect
	• The effects and gifts of the Spirit presuppose the presence of the Spirit
	• The Spirit is renewed and made like to God through its operation

â€¢ ••Works of the Law vs. Hearing of Faith••
	• "Works of the law" refers to justification by the works of the law
	• "Hearing of faith" refers to the doctrine of justification by faith in Christ crucified
	• Faith signifies both the gift of believing and the doctrine believed

â€¢ ••Spirit and Flesh••
	• "Spirit" refers to the operation of the Spirit, renewing and making the inward man like to God
	• "Flesh" refers to outward things or actions that pertain to the outward man
	• "Flesh" and "new creature" are opposed, with the new creature being the inward man renewed by the Spirit

â€¢ ••The Resolution••
	• Paul argues that if the Galatians received the Spirit by his doctrine, his doctrine is true
	• Adding justification by works to the gospel is foolish, as it contradicts the doctrine of justification by faith

â€¢ ••The Use••
	• Paul notes the fault of the Galatians and others who become proud and think themselves wiser than their teachers
	• We must join knowledge of ourselves with knowledge of God's word and mix knowledge with love

â€¢ ••The Difference between Law and Gospel••
	• The law does not minister the Spirit, but only shows our disease and gives no remedy
	• The gospel ministers the Spirit, showing what we are to do and giving the Spirit to make us do it

â€¢ ••The Necessity of Preaching the Gospel••
	• The preaching of the gospel is necessary for all men, as it is the instrument of God to confer the Spirit
	• Even the most learned need the Spirit of God to guide and govern them

â€¢ ••The Scope of Hearing and Teaching••
	• The scope of all our hearing and teaching is to receive the Spirit of God
	• Without the Spirit, we can do nothing

â€¢ ••An Infallible Argument for Scripture••
	• The Scriptures have a divine and supernatural operation in their right use (reading, hearing, meditation)
	• This is an infallible argument for the truth of Scripture

â€¢ ••The Importance of the Spirit••
	• The Spirit is necessary for all men, and even the most learned need it to guide and govern them
	• The Spirit is the key to understanding and applying God's word.

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

â€¢ ••True Religion••
	• Stands in the Spirit, not in outward ceremonies or performances
	• Involves a divine instruction that guides the heart and inner man
	• Requires a new creation in Christ, not just outward observance

â€¢ ••Mark of True Religion••
	• The preaching of true religion confers the Spirit of adoption
	• Assures believers that they are children of God, not in suspense of salvation
	• Does not minister the spirit of pride, cruelty, or presumption

â€¢ ••Papist Religion••
	• Does not confer the Spirit of adoption, but teaches suspense of salvation
	• Ministers the spirit of pride and presumption through human satisfactions and merits
	• Has been associated with wars, seditions, and rebellions in Europe

â€¢ ••God's Service••
	• Stands in justice, peace of conscience, and joy in the Holy Ghost
	• Requires a new creation in Christ, not just outward observance
	• Involves a good conscience that makes promises to God

â€¢ ••Fault of the World••
	• Places religion in ceremonial performances and outward duties
	• Fails to look at the heart and inner man, which is the true seat of religion

â€¢ ••Outward Shows vs. Inner Reality••
	• Outward shows and ceremonies may not be condemned, but are not the power and life of religion
	• True religion lies in the heart and inner man, not just in outward observance

â€¢ ••Prayer and Religion••
	• True prayer involves praying in the heart unto God by the Spirit of prayer
	• Approving one's heart unto God is essential for any act of religion

â€¢ ••After Proceedings in Religion••
	• Must be answerable to our first beginnings in the Spirit
	• Requires a consistent and ongoing commitment to the Spirit

â€¢ ••Folly of Popish Religion••
	• Begins in God's mercy and the merit of Christ, but ends in human merits and satisfactions
	• Fails to maintain a consistent commitment to the Spirit

â€¢ ••Common Sin of Our Times••
	• We are deceived in our practice of religion and have fallen away from our former zeal
	• We must amend this fault and grow in good things, lest we be spewed out by God.

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

â€¢ ••Growing in the Spirit••
	• Aged persons who have begun in the Spirit should grow up in the graces of the Spirit.
	• They should end in the Spirit, with their love, service, and works increasing over time.
	• This is seen in the example of the angel of Thyatira, who had more love, service, and works at the end than at the beginning.

â€¢ ••The Importance of Spiritual Growth••
	• Aged persons who do not grow in the Spirit lose their honor.
	• Age is a crown of glory when it is found in the way of righteousness.
	• Aged persons should pray to God not to forsake them in their old age.

â€¢ ••The Futility of Suffering••
	• If believers have suffered for the gospel but have now revolted from it, their sufferings are in vain.
	• However, if they repent, their sufferings will not be in vain.

â€¢ ••The Consequence of Revolting from the Gospel••
	• If believers received the Spirit by Paul's doctrine, then his doctrine is true, and they are fools for revolting from it.
	• Their sufferings, which were once meaningful, are now in vain.

â€¢ ••The Call to Suffering••
	• Believers are called to be bearers and sufferers in this life.
	• This is because they are called to resign all revenge to God and imitate the passion of Christ.

â€¢ ••The Purpose of Suffering••
	• Suffering is for our good, as it helps us to grow in our faith and character.
	• It is a blessing to suffer for righteousness.

â€¢ ••The Extent of Suffering••
	• Believers may be called to suffer even to the shedding of their blood, if it is for the resisting of sin.

â€¢ ••God's Faithfulness••
	• God is faithful and will not lay on us more than we shall be able to bear.
	• This should encourage believers to trust in God and not make reckoning of pleasures and delights in this world.

â€¢ ••The Gospel is Not a Gospel of Ease••
	• The gospel is not a gospel of ease, but rather a call to suffer and bear for the sake of righteousness.
	• Believers should not make reckoning of pleasures and delights in this world, but rather seek to please God.

â€¢ ••The Example of Paul••
	• Paul commends himself by the multitude of his sufferings, which he endured for the sake of the gospel.
	• This shows that suffering is a normal part of the Christian life, and that it is a blessing to suffer for righteousness.

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

â€¢ ••The Call to Suffering••
	• We must take up our own cross, which is the particular affliction and misery God lays on us (Luke 9:23)
	• This means being content to bear and put up with wrongs and abuses in dissensions and differences

â€¢ ••The Reality of Suffering••
	• We may suffer in this world by condition, and must be prepared for trials and afflictions
	• The harvest of the Lord has been among us for over 40 years, and now is the time of threshing, fanning, and grinding

â€¢ ••The Gift of Suffering••
	• Power to suffer is a gift from God (Phil. 1:29)
	• We must pray for this gift and observe the commandment of God not to fear the terror of men (1 Peter 3:14)

â€¢ ••The Purpose of Suffering••
	• Our sufferings are of great use, unless our sins are the hindrance
	• We must show all patience and longsuffering, and that with joyfulness (Col. 1:11)

â€¢ ••The Uses of Suffering••
	• They serve as a trial of men to reveal what is hidden in their hearts (Deut. 8:2)
	• They serve as a correction of things amiss in us (1 Cor. 11:32)
	• They serve as documents and warnings to others, especially in public persons
	• They are marks of adoption, if we are content to obey God in them (Heb. 12:7)
	• They are the trodden and beaten way to the kingdom of heaven (Acts 14:22)

â€¢ ••The Moderation of Reproof••
	• Paul reproves and terrifies the Galatians, yet preserves the hope of mercy in them
	• The prophets also practice moderation in their reproofs and censures

â€¢ ••The Ministry of the Gospel••
	• The minister of the gospel works miracles among us, but not by the works of the law, but by the hearing of faith
	• This is a repetition of the second verse, and the exposition must be fetched from there

â€¢ ••The Importance of Faith••
	• Faith is the key to understanding the ministry of the gospel and the purpose of suffering
	• We must have faith in God's presence, protection, and providence

â€¢ ••The Call to Patience••
	• We must show all patience and longsuffering, and that with joyfulness (Col. 1:11)
	• This means being content to bear and put up with wrongs and abuses in dissensions and differences

â€¢ ••The Reward of Suffering••
	• Our sufferings can be a means of sanctification and a way to the kingdom of heaven (Acts 14:22)
	• We must be willing to obey God in our sufferings and trust in His presence, protection, and providence.

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

â€¢ ••The Spirit of Adoption and Extraordinary Gifts••
	• The Galatians received the Spirit of adoption and other extraordinary gifts, such as speaking in tongues and healing diseases.
	• These gifts are not idle, but rather emphasize the importance and certainty of the thing being repeated.

â€¢ ••The Argument for Infallible Assurance••
	• The argument is that if the Galatians received the Spirit by Paul's doctrine, then it is true and of God.
	• This leads to an infallible assurance of the certainty of the Scriptures and true religion.

â€¢ ••Falls of Infirmity and the Spirit••
	• Falls of infirmity in the child of God do not utterly extinguish the Spirit, but rather grieve or make sad the Spirit.
	• This shows that the Spirit is not lost, but rather temporarily affected.

â€¢ ••God as the Author of Miracles••
	• God is the only and proper author of miracles.
	• Miracles are works above the strength of nature, and can only be effected by the author of nature.

â€¢ ••The Gift of Miracles••
	• The gift of miracles was not given to men to work and effect miracles, but rather to have faith in miracles.
	• The faith was grounded on revelation, and the revelation was that God would work the miracle when prayed or commanded.

â€¢ ••The Devil's Limitations••
	• The devil can work wonders and extraordinary things, but not true miracles that exceed the strength of nature.
	• The devil cannot make rain, thunder, or lightning, and cannot create new creatures.

â€¢ ••The Use of Miracles••
	• Miracles were used to confirm doctrine in the apostolic churches.
	• Their use is not limited to the past, but can be used today to confirm doctrine.

â€¢ ••The Ease of Falling Away••
	• The Galatians fell away from Paul's doctrine despite receiving the Spirit of adoption and being enabled to work miracles.
	• This shows how easy it is to fall from God and our faith.

â€¢ ••Rules for Maintaining Faith••
	• We must not have a conceit of anything in us, but rather hold our faith and religion in fear, as in the presence of God.
	• We must take heed that there be not an evil, corrupt, and dissembling heart, for if our heart is naught, our faith cannot be good.

â€¢ ••Abraham's Faith••
	• Abraham's faith was imputed to him, and he believed God, which is an example of faith that is pleasing to God.

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

â€¢ ••Paul's Argument••
	• Paul argues that those who are of faith are the children of Abraham
	• He uses Abraham's example to prove the truth of his doctrine of justification by faith without works

â€¢ ••Abraham's Justification••
	• Abraham was justified by justice imputed and apprehended by faith
	• This is the main ground for the justification of a sinner in the Old and New Testament

â€¢ ••Interpretations of Abraham's Faith••
	• Some interpret Abraham's faith as virtue and goodness in the eyes of men
	• However, this is not sufficient to acquit and justify us before God

â€¢ ••Papist Interpretations••
	• Papists interpret faith as a general faith that believes the articles of faith
	• They also understand imputation as reputation, where faith is esteemed as it is indeed

â€¢ ••Defects in Papist Interpretations••
	• Charity is not the form or life of faith, but the fruit and effect of it
	• Works are the life of pretended faith or profession of faith

â€¢ ••Faith and Justice••
	• Faith is not the whole and entire justice before God
	• Love cannot be included in the act of believing

â€¢ ••Faith and Charity••
	• Faith joined with charity is not the justice whereby a sinner is justified
	• Our faith and love are both imperfect

â€¢ ••Paul's View of Faith••
	• Paul says that the righteousness whereby we are justified is "by" or "through faith"
	• Our justice and our faith are two different things

â€¢ ••The True Sense of the Words••
	• Paul considers two things: Abraham's faith and the fruit of his faith
	• He examines the occasion of Abraham's faith and the comfort God gave him

â€¢ ••The Fruit of Abraham's Faith••
	• The fruit of Abraham's faith was that it was imputed to him for righteousness
	• This is the key to understanding the true sense of the words

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

â€¢ ••Abraham's Faith••
	• Abraham's faith is renewed when God renews and enlarges His promise to him
	• Abraham's faith is an example for us to renew our faith in times of fear and danger

â€¢ ••Object of Abraham's Faith••
	• The object of Abraham's faith is the multiplication of his posterity
	• This promise is dependent on a more principal promise: God's favor and the incarnation of the Son of God

â€¢ ••Property of Abraham's Faith••
	• Abraham's faith is a faith against hope, as he believed in a promise when his body was half dead and Sarai was barren
	• We are to have a similar faith, believing in the promise of remission of sins and life everlasting despite our temptations and infirmities

â€¢ ••Effect and Fruit of Abraham's Faith••
	• The first point is imputation, which means reckoning or keeping a reckoning of expenses and receipts
	• Imputation is a speech borrowed from merchants, signifying God's judgment of men for their sins

â€¢ ••Imputation••
	• Imputation is twofold: legal and evangelical
	• Legal imputation is when God wills and adjudges the reward to him that fulfills the law
	• Evangelical imputation is when God accepts the satisfaction of Christ as a payment for our sins

â€¢ ••What is Imputed?••
	• Faith is imputed, but not as a quality in itself, but as an instrument or hand holding and receiving Christ
	• The act of believing is not our justice, but the thing believed is imputed to us by God

â€¢ ••Righteousness••
	• Righteousness is the justice of God, which is sufficient to acquit a sinner at the bar of God's judgment
	• The sense is that Abraham believed in the obedience of the mediator (the blessed seed), which was accepted by God as his obedience for his justification

â€¢ ••Imputation of Christ's Obedience••
	• The obedience of Christ is to be imputed to us for our justification
	• This is the central point of the text, and the objection that the obedience of Christ is not to be imputed is addressed

â€¢ ••Faith as an Instrument••
	• Faith is an instrument or hand holding and receiving Christ
	• Believing is put for the thing believed, and the act of believing is not our justice, but the thing believed is imputed to us by God

â€¢ ••Justification by Faith••
	• Justification is not by our works, but by faith in the obedience of Christ
	• The text emphasizes the importance of faith in receiving Christ and being justified by God.

Here are 10 concise bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

â€¢ ••Imputation of Christ's work••
	• Christ's work is imputed to us, making us just before God
	• This is the basis of our justification, not our own works

â€¢ ••Justice of the person vs. justice of the act••
	• Phinehas' action was just, but not his person
	• The psalm speaks only of the justice of his action, not his person

â€¢ ••Imputed justice in the church••
	• Imputed justice was known in the church from the early days
	• Fathers such as Augustine and Jerome taught that justice is imputed to us through Christ

â€¢ ••No merit in man's works••
	• Faith alone is imputed for justice, not works
	• Our works do not contribute to our justification

â€¢ ••One justification, not two••
	• There is only one justification, through faith in Christ
	• The idea of a second justification through works is a fiction

â€¢ ••The obedience of Christ••
	• The obedience of Christ is imputed to us, making us just before God
	• This is the basis of our salvation

â€¢ ••Our duty before God••
	• We must acknowledge ourselves as bankrupts and plead for pardon
	• We must accept the satisfaction of Christ for our justification

â€¢ ••Children of Abraham••
	• To be of faith is to believe and apply the promise of righteousness through Christ
	• This is the definition of being a child of Abraham

â€¢ ••Two sorts of children of Abraham••
	• Some are children of Abraham by nature, through natural generation
	• Others are children of Abraham by grace, through faith in Christ

â€¢ ••Believers as children of Abraham••
	• Believers are children of Abraham by imitation, in that they follow his faith
	• Believers are also children of Abraham by succession, in that they inherit the promise of righteousness through Christ

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

â€¢ ••Believers are children of Abraham••
	• Through spiritual generation, not physical descent
	• By believing the promise of a seed, Abraham begets them spiritually
	• Properly, the promise and election of God makes them children

â€¢ ••The true mark of a child of Abraham••
	• To be of the faith of Abraham
	• Not just to descend from Isaac or Hagar, but to follow Abraham's faith

â€¢ ••The faith of Abraham is essential••
	• Corrupted by the papist, and not sufficient for salvation
	• Must be demonstrated by good evidence, not just profession or tradition

â€¢ ••Three things to labor for••
	• Knowledge of the main promise and its scope
	• Belief in the power and truth of God in accomplishing the promise
	• Obedience to God through faith, shutting out worldly desires

â€¢ ••The love of worldly things hinders faith••
	• Love of honor, pleasures, and riches takes precedence over faith
	• Faith takes no place where these desires are present

â€¢ ••Professed faith is often false••
	• Conceived without the means of the word, prayer, and sacraments
	• Joined with the purpose to live as one pleases, rather than to obey God

â€¢ ••The faith of Abraham is proven by Scripture••
	• Paul cites the Galatians to demonstrate the truth of being a child of Abraham
	• Requires a measure of knowledge and understanding of Scripture

â€¢ ••The contempt of knowledge in our days••
	• Most men reject the preaching of the gospel and rely on natural teaching
	• Reject the authority of Scripture and the teaching of the apostles

â€¢ ••The importance of understanding Scripture••
	• Must be able to judge the gathering of doctrine from Scripture
	• Requires a deep understanding of the Bible and its teachings

â€¢ ••The need for true faith and obedience••
	• Must labor to be of the faith of Abraham and walk in his steps
	• Must obey God through faith, shutting out worldly desires and following His will.

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

â€¢ ••God's Foreknowledge••
	• God's foreknowledge is joined with His will and decree
	• Foreknowledge is not a bare prediction, but a consequence of God's will
	• God's will and foreknowledge are not separate, but intertwined

â€¢ ••Order of Foreknowledge and Will••
	• Foreknowledge of possible events comes before God's will
	• Foreknowledge of certain events follows God's will and decree
	• Things come to pass because they are to come to pass, according to God's will

â€¢ ••Who Foresees?••
	• The Scripture records God's foreknowledge
	• God's foreknowledge is revealed through the writings of Moses and Paul
	• The writings of Moses and Paul are the Word of God

â€¢ ••What is Foreseen?••
	• God's foreknowledge includes the justification of the Gentiles
	• God's decree is to justify all Gentiles in the last age of the world
	• The text of Paul must be understood in this context

â€¢ ••God's Decree••
	• God's decree is not to redeem and justify all men of all ages and times
	• The decree is only to justify all Gentiles in the last age of the world
	• This is in line with 1 Timothy 2:4, "God would have all men to be saved"

â€¢ ••The Manner of God's Speech••
	• God preached the gospel to Abraham
	• The gospel was known to the prophets and apostles
	• The antiquity of the gospel is evident in the testimony of God to Abraham

â€¢ ••The Substance of True Religion••
	• True religion was known to the prophets and apostles
	• The substance of true religion was known to Abraham
	• The gospel was preached to Abraham, showing the antiquity of the gospel

â€¢ ••Justification by Faith••
	• Those who are of faith are blessed with faithful Abraham
	• Justification by faith is not limited to Jews, but also includes Gentiles
	• The Gentiles are blessed of God as Abraham was

â€¢ ••The Argument Against the Doubt••
	• The doubt was that only Jews who are of faith are justified
	• Paul removes this doubt by showing that God's promise to Abraham includes the Gentiles
	• The justification of the Gentiles is a consequence of God's decree

â€¢ ••The Significance of God's Speech to Abraham••
	• God's speech to Abraham is a testimony to the gospel
	• The gospel was preached to Abraham, showing its antiquity
	• The substance of true religion was known to Abraham, and is now revealed through the writings of Paul.

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

â€¢ ••The doctrine of justification by faith without works is ancient••
	• The true way of life and doctrine of justification by faith without works is old
	• Papists claim antiquity for their religion, but their proper points and heads were taken up long after Christ's time

â€¢ ••The speech or testimony of God to Abraham••
	• God says, "In thee shall all the Gentiles be blessed" (Gen. 22:18)
	• This means that Gentiles are blessed through Christ, who is in Abraham's loins

â€¢ ••The meaning of "all the Gentiles"••
	• Abraham is called the father of many nations (Gen. 17:4)
	• He is the father of all Gentiles in regard to their faith, not just their flesh

â€¢ ••The benediction mentioned in Scripture••
	• Comprehends all spiritual graces of God, including vocation, justification, and glorification (Eph. 1:3)

â€¢ ••The use of the benediction••
	• The Lord says that all nations shall be blessed in Abraham
	• This means that the nation of the Jews will be called and converted to the participation of this blessing

â€¢ ••The verification of the benediction••
	• The English nation and many other nations are blessed in this seed of Abraham
	• We are admonished to give thanks and praise, amend our lives, and believe in Christ

â€¢ ••The call to bless all and do good to all••
	• We must bless all, do good to all, and hurt to none (1 Peter 3:9)
	• We are heirs of blessing and must live accordingly

â€¢ ••The comfort of turning to God••
	• If we truly turn to God and believe in the holy seed of Abraham, all things shall go well with us
	• God will bless those who bless us and curse those who curse us (Gen. 12:3)

â€¢ ••The conclusion of Paul's answer••
	• All men that are of Abraham's faith, though foreigners and Gentiles, shall be partakers of the same blessing of God with him

â€¢ ••The second reason for justification by faith••
	• Those who are of works are under the curse (Gal. 3:10)
	• Therefore, those who are of faith are blessed or justified with Abraham

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

â€¢ ••The Curse of the Law••
	• Those who do not fulfill the law are cursed (Galatians 3:10)
	• Those who rely on works to fulfill the law are also cursed

â€¢ ••The World's Perspective••
	• Most people believe they can be saved by their good deeds
	• This is a conclusion of nature, but it is a false one

â€¢ ••The Jews' Perspective••
	• The Jews believed they could be justified by their own righteousness
	• They established their own righteousness based on the law (Romans 10:3)

â€¢ ••The Papacy's Perspective••
	• The papacy teaches justification by works, which is a path to perdition
	• This is in contrast to the Christian faith, which teaches justification by faith in Christ

â€¢ ••The Christian Perspective••
	• Christians are justified by faith in the blood of Christ
	• This is the safest and surest way to avoid danger

â€¢ ••The Curse••
	• The curse is eternal woe and misery
	• It can be experienced in this life, at the end of this life, or in the life to come

â€¢ ••The Curse in This Life••
	• The curse can be within a person (e.g. ignorance, difficulty learning, pain)
	• The curse can also be without a person (e.g. spiritual bondage, enmity with creatures, losses and calamities)

â€¢ ••The Curse Within a Person••
	• Ignorance of God, self, and true happiness
	• Difficulty learning and retaining things
	• Pain and manifold accusations in the conscience
	• Inclination to sin and hardness of heart
	• Diseases in the body

â€¢ ••The Curse Without a Person••
	• Spiritual bondage under the power of the devil
	• Enmity with all creatures since the fall
	• Losses, calamities, and miseries in goods, friends, and reputation

â€¢ ••The Curse After This Life••
	• The second death, which is separation of body and soul from God
	• A full apprehension of the wrath of God
	• Eternal torment and misery

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

â€¢ ••The Curse of the Law••
	• The curse of the law is a serious and absolute warning
	• It is a reminder of the consequences of sin and the importance of obedience
	• Men should think and speak more about it to have a conscience of sin

â€¢ ••Who is Cursed••
	• Anyone who does not obey all the commandments of the law is cursed
	• Breaking one commandment makes one guilty of all
	• God will not accept partial obedience, but demands all or nothing

â€¢ ••The Nature of the Curse••
	• The curse is not just for those who break the law, but also for those who do not continue in all things
	• Breaking the law even once, or in one thought, is enough to incur the curse
	• The curse is not just for the act of sin, but also for the attitude of disobedience

â€¢ ••The Consequences of the Curse••
	• The curse is a horrible and irreversible sentence
	• There is no way to escape or relieve oneself from the curse
	• The whole world and all things in it belong to God, and no one can hide from His presence

â€¢ ••The Way of Escape••
	• The only way to escape the curse is to flee to the throne of grace for mercy
	• Instantly ask, seek, and knock for pardon of sins
	• Encouragement comes from the fact that sins are pardoned at the first purpose to amend

â€¢ ••The Gospel Exception••
	• The threatening of the law must be understood with an exception made by the gospel
	• The gospel adds an exception: "except he repent"
	• This exception is seen in the story of Jonah and the people of Nineveh

â€¢ ••Forgiveness without Limitation••
	• Forgiveness is promised without any limitation to the number or kinds of sin
	• The only exception is the sin against the Holy Ghost
	• Sinners can appeal with boldness to the throne of grace for forgiveness

â€¢ ••When is a Sinner Accursed?••
	• A sinner is accursed in the present time, in this life
	• The Lord says "He is accursed", not "He shall be accursed"
	• There are those who think themselves safe from danger, but are deceived

â€¢ ••The Deception of Sinners••
	• Some sinners think they are safe from danger because the time to the last judgment is long
	• But they are deceived, and the sentence of the law is already given
	• The halter of judgment is already around their necks, waiting to be turned

â€¢ ••The Importance of Repentance••
	• Repentance is the only way to escape the curse
	• Sinners must flee to the throne of grace for mercy and pardon
	• The gospel offers forgiveness without limitation, except for the sin against the Holy Ghost.

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

• ••The Impossibility of Fulfilling the Law••
	• The execution of the law is already in blindness of mind and hardness of heart (John 3)
	• No one can fulfill the law within themselves in this life
• ••Paul's Argument••
	• If we could fulfill the law, we might be justified by the law (Rom. 7:14-24)
	• But no man can be justified by the law or by works
	• Therefore, no man can fulfill the law
• ••The Nature of the Law••
	• The law is spiritual and requires inward and spiritual obedience
	• Paul admits that he was carnal and not conformable to the law
• ••The Limitations of Human Knowledge and Love••
	• We know God only in part, and therefore we love in part
	• We do not fulfill the law because of our limited knowledge and love
• ••The Universality of Sin••
	• All men, even the regenerate, are considered sinners to the very death
	• Our righteousness is as a defiled cloth (Isa. 64:6)
• ••The Papist Error••
	• The papists say that all men can fulfill the law in this life, but this is blasphemy
	• There are no venial sins that are not against the law of God
• ••The Role of Christ••
	• If we were perfectly sanctified and fulfilled the law, then Christ would not be a Savior
	• Christ is necessary because we are unable to fulfill the law on our own
• ••The Impossibility of Fulfilling the Law in This Life••
	• Man by creation could love God with all the powers of his soul, but now no man can do this
	• It is impossible for any man in the time of this life to fulfill the law
• ••The Use of This Doctrine••
	• This doctrine serves to condemn the folly of the world
	• It serves to confute sundry errors of the papists
• ••The Papist Error (continued)••
	• The papists say that a man may fulfill the law in this life, but this is based on a false assumption
	• They say that there is a double degree of fulfilling the law, but this is not supported by Scripture

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

â€¢ ••Interpretation of "with all thy heart"••
	• Can be taken in a double signification
	• Opposed to a double heart, signifying a true upright heart
	• Can also signify giving all power of the soul and all strength to obey the law

â€¢ ••Perfection of parts vs. perfection of degrees••
	• Perfection of parts: having the beginnings of virtues and seeds of graces
	• Perfection of degrees: fulfilling the law in matter and manner
	• The former speaks of perfection of parts, acknowledging imperfection

â€¢ ••Fulfilling the law••
	• There are two kinds: legal and evangelical
	• Legal: doing all things required in the law, only possible for Christ and Adam
	• Evangelical: believing in Christ and endeavoring to obey God's precepts

â€¢ ••Righteousness of good conscience••
	• An endeavor to obey God's precepts, joined with the purpose of not sinning
	• Accepted by God as a fulfilling of the law in those who are in Christ

â€¢ ••Praying to fulfill the law••
	• We pray to strive to attain to the fulfilling of the law, not to be equal to angels and saints
	• We desire to imitate them and be like them

â€¢ ••The will and the law••
	• Paul's will was partly renewed and partly unrenewed
	• He could not perfectly will what is good, and partly willed evil

â€¢ ••Christ and the law••
	• Christ took our flesh to fulfill the righteousness of the law in us
	• The righteousness of the law is fulfilled in us through faith in Christ

â€¢ ••Love and the law••
	• Love is the fulfilling of the law (Rom. 13:8)
	• Love is the key to fulfilling the law, not just doing what is required

â€¢ ••David and the law••
	• David turned from nothing that God commanded him, except in the matter of Uriah
	• He fulfilled the law in an evangelical manner, through faith and endeavor

â€¢ ••Zachary and Elisabeth and the law••
	• They walked in all the commandments of God, without reproof
	• They fulfilled the law in an evangelical manner, through faith and endeavor

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

• ••Objection VIII: Born of God sins not••
	• The objection is that if someone is born of God, they do not sin.
	• The answer is that this means they do not commit sin habitually, but may still fall into sin occasionally.
• ••Objection IX: Commandments of God are not grievous••
	• The objection is that the commandments of God are not burdensome.
	• The answer is that they are not grievous in three ways:
		- In respect of remission (because Christ's obedience is imputed to believers)
		- In respect of imputation (because Christ's obedience is imputed to believers)
		- In respect of inchoation (because believers receive the Spirit of God to enable them to obey)
• ••Objection X: Works of God are perfect••
	• The objection is that good works are perfect because they are works of God.
	• The answer is that works that are purely God's work are perfect, but good works that involve human effort are imperfect.
• ••Objection XI: God gives impossible law••
	• The objection is that if God gives an impossible law, He is cruel.
	• The answer is that God gave the law with the power to fulfill it, and the law is impossible only because humans have lost this power.
• ••The law is impossible to fulfill••
	• The conclusion is that the law is impossible to fulfill in this life.
	• This means that we must seek to fulfill the law through Christ, who is the end of the law for righteousness.
• ••Justification by imputation••
	• The conclusion is that our justification must be by the imputation of Christ's justice to us.
• ••The law as a glass••
	• The law must be a glass to show us our impotency and what we cannot do.
	• This drives us to Christ and to prayer for the Spirit to enable us to obey the law.
• ••Pleasing God••
	• The question is how our works can please God if we cannot fulfill the law.
	• The answer is that every good work has something that is God's and something that is ours alone, and God approves the good part of the work.
• ••Boasting must be laid aside••
	• The conclusion is that after we begin to please God in obedience to His laws, we must lay aside all boasting of our goodness.
	• We must humble ourselves under God's hand, even to the point of death.
• ••The law is not of faith••
	• The conclusion is that the law is not of faith, but rather a means of living.
	• The just person lives by faith, not by the law.

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

â€¢ ••Paul's Disputation••
	• Paul's argument is not just about ceremonial law, but about the whole law
	• He argues that if moral duties are necessary for justification, then the law is necessary

â€¢ ••The Law in Justification••
	• The law can be considered in two ways: as justifying without Christ or with Christ
	• Paul argues that the law is excluded from justification in both cases

â€¢ ••The Scope of the Text••
	• The text "The just shall live by faith" is from Habakkuk 2:4
	• Paul applies this text to his purpose, saying that life eternal comes by faith

â€¢ ••The Law and Faith••
	• The law does not prescribe faith in the Messiah
	• The law promises life to those who do the things contained in the law, not to those who believe in the Messiah

â€¢ ••Justice by Faith••
	• Justice is by faith, not by the law
	• The law does not justify by faith, therefore it does not justify at all

â€¢ ••Justification Before God and Men••
	• Justification before God is when God reputes a man just for the merit and obedience of Christ
	• Justification before men is when those who profess faith in Christ are reputed just by men

â€¢ ••Reconciling Paul and James••
	• Paul and James are reconciled by understanding that they are speaking of different types of justification
	• Paul speaks of justification before God, while James speaks of justification before men

â€¢ ••Double Election••
	• There is a special election, where God knows who are his
	• There is also a general election, where we repute all men to be elect who profess faith in Christ

â€¢ ••Double Sanctification••
	• There is a sanctification before God, in truth
	• There is also a sanctification before men, in the judgment of charity

â€¢ ••Baptism and Sanctification••
	• Those who are baptized are holy and regenerate in the judgment of charity
	• This is not the same as the judgment of certainty, which is God's secret knowledge

â€¢ ••God's Judgment••
	• There is a universal judgment of God before whom we must all appear
	• This judgment is already begun upon us, even in this life
	• We must walk in godly and holy conversation, in the fear of God

â€¢ ••Living by Faith••
	• We must choose the true God as our God
	• We must cleave unto Him, according to His word
	• We must have the eye of knowledge to direct our steps in the ways of God

â€¢ ••Temptations and Dangers••
	• We will be assailed with many temptations on the right hand and on the left

	• Our faith must be a hand to stay us in times of temptation

â€¢ ••Faith and Justification••
	• The just man lives by faith
	• True faith is always joined with the purpose of not sinning or with the justice of good conscience
	• The second justification by works is a mere fiction

â€¢ ••The Law and the Gospel••
	• The law promises life to him that performs perfect obedience
	• The gospel promises life to him that believes in Christ, yet not for his faith, but for the merit of Christ

â€¢ ••Objections to the Gospel••
	• Objection I: The gospel requires repentance and the practice of it
		- Answer: The law does not teach true repentance, but only an occasion for it
	• Objection II: The law requires and commands faith
		- Answer: The law requires faith in God, but the gospel requires faith in Christ, the mediator
	• Objection III: The gospel has promises of life upon condition of our obedience
		- Answer: The gospel promises life to those who believe in Christ, not for their obedience, but for the merit of Christ

â€¢ ••The Gospel Promises••
	• The promises of the gospel are made to the worker, not the work

â€¢ ••Faith as a Virtue••
	• Faith is considered an instrument or hand to apprehend Christ

â€¢ ••The Law and the Gospel••
	• The Church of Rome has confused the law and the gospel for centuries
	• The law written in our hearts is still the law of Moses, not the gospel
	• The distinction between the law and the gospel is crucial for understanding salvation

â€¢ ••Justification and Works••
	• Believing and doing are opposed in the article of justification
	• Faith goes before, and doing follows in good conversation
	• In justification, faith and doing are like fire and water, opposing each other

â€¢ ••Special Faith••
	• A special and applying faith is required for justification
	• General faith is considered a work of the law and is not sufficient for justification

â€¢ ••Works of Faith and Grace••
	• Works of faith and grace are excluded from justification
	• The opposition is between believing and doing, not between believing and the works of nature

â€¢ ••The Law and Human Weakness••

â€¢ ••Full Redemption••
	• To those who believe, there is full redemption from the curse of the law
	• This redemption is made possible through Christ's work on the cross

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

• ••Christ's Accusation and Curse••
	• Christ took upon Himself the punishment of our sin on the cross.
	• He was made a curse, but as the Son of God, He was not accursed in His divine nature.
	• He was accursed in His human nature, specifically in His flesh and soul.
• ••Nature of Christ's Accusation••
	• The curse was not on the Godhead of Christ, but only in His flesh or manhood.
	• The soul of Christ was the principal seat of the curse, as it was the principal seat of sin.
• ••Extent of Christ's Accusation••
	• Christ entered into the first degree of death, but not the second.
	• The first degree of death is separation from God in sense and seeing.
	• The second degree of death is an absolute separation from God, which Christ did not experience.
• ••Christ's Suffering of God's Wrath••
	• Christ suffered the wrath of God willingly and of His own accord.
	• He felt the wrath of God, but it had no dominion or lordship over Him.
• ••Value of Christ's Suffering••
	• The punishment Christ suffered was in value and measure answerable to all the sins of all the elect past, present, and to come.
	• The Godhead supported the manhood of Christ, enabling Him to bear and overcome the whole burden of God's wrath.
• ••Objections to Christ's Suffering••
	• Objection 1: The Scripture ascribes all to the blood of Christ, and therefore to the death of the body.
		- Answer: By blood is meant a bloody death, specifically the death of the cross.
	• Objection 2: The suffering of the anger of God was not figured in sacraments or sacrifices.
		- Answer: The burnt offering was a type of Christ's sacrifice, where the beast was tied to the altar, shed, and then burned unto God.

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

â€¢ ••Objection III: Temporal death vs. Eternal death••
	• The objection is that temporal death cannot countervail eternal death.
	• The answer is that Christ, being God and man, suffered infinite punishment, but not eternally.

â€¢ ••Christ's suffering••
	• Christ's suffering was infinite, but not eternal.
	• The dignity of Christ's person makes His suffering more significant than if all men had suffered eternal death.

â€¢ ••Objection IV: Christ suffering the pains of hell••
	• The objection is that it's hard to say Christ suffered the pains of hell.
	• The answer is that Christ could not be held by the sorrows of hell, and His suffering was not contrary to the purity of His manhood.

â€¢ ••The Use of Christ's curse••
	• The curse of Christ shows the greatness and horribleness of human sins.
	• It shows the unspeakable love and mercy of God.
	• It should lead us to acknowledge, consider, and bewail our sins.

â€¢ ••The effect of Christ's curse••
	• Those who believe in Christ's curse are redeemed from the curse of the law.
	• They should die to their sins and live to God.
	• Many who profess Christ make no change in their lives due to secret atheism.

â€¢ ••Obedience to God••
	• Christ's obedience to His Father in bearing the curse of the law teaches us to subject ourselves to God's will.
	• Our obedience should include suffering the miseries laid on us to the death.

â€¢ ••The confirmation of Christ's curse••
	• The sentence and decree of God confirm that Christ was accursed (Deut. 21:23).
	• The ground of this sentence is the sin of the malefactor, for whom God curses.

â€¢ ••Why the man hanged on the tree is cursed••
	• The man hanged on the tree is cursed because he was a grievous malefactor.
	• Hanging was a most odious and infamous death.
	• God foresaw that the Messiah would die on the cross, and therefore accursed this kind of death.

â€¢ ••No fault or offense in Christ••
	• If there was no fault or offense in Christ, He could not be accursed.
	• However, Christ's death was a fulfillment of the sentence and decree of God.

â€¢ ••The significance of Christ's curse••
	• Christ's curse shows the greatness and horribleness of human sins.
	• It shows the unspeakable love and mercy of God.
	• It should lead us to acknowledge, consider, and bewail our sins.

â€¢ ••The Curse of the Thief••
	• The thief who repented was not accursed, despite being hanged on the tree.

â€¢ ••The Covenant Cannot be Abrogated••
	• The covenant or testament confirmed by God cannot be changed or abolished.
	• The promise cannot be made ineffective.

â€¢ ••The Promise is a Covenant••
	• The promise made to Abraham is a covenant or compact between God and humanity.
	• God promises remission of sins and life everlasting, and requires faith in return.

â€¢ ••The Promise is a Testament••
	• The promise is a will or testament in two respects:
		- The promise is confirmed by the death of the mediator (Christ).
		- The things promised are given freely, without our desert or procurement.

â€¢ ••The Promise is a Legacy••
	• Remission of sins and life everlasting are a legacy given to those who believe.
	• We must bring nothing to God but a desire for these things and ask for them.

â€¢ ••The Covenant was Confirmed by Oath••
	• The covenant was confirmed by God's oath (Hebrews 6:17).
	• This oath was given to Abraham, the father of all the faithful.

â€¢ ••The Covenant was Confirmed to Abraham and His Seed••
	• The covenant was confirmed to Abraham, and then to his seed (Christ and all believers).
	• This means that the covenant is available to all believers, whether Jew or Gentile.

â€¢ ••God's Goodness is Shown in the Covenant••
	• God's goodness is shown in the fact that He ratifies His promise by oath.
	• This shows that God is willing to go to great lengths to confirm His promise.

â€¢ ••We Can Have Sure Hope of Life Everlasting••
	• Because God has spoken to us through Abraham.
	• Because God has made promise of blessing to us.
	• Because God has sworn unto us.

	• The covenant encourages us to all diligence in using good means to attain to the condition of Abraham.
	• The covenant also arms us to all patience in bearing the miseries and calamities of the strait way to eternal life.

â€¢ ••Christ is the Ground of the Promise••
	• Christ is the scope and foundation of all the promises of God.
	• Christ procured remission of sins and life everlasting by His death and passion.
	• Christ seals up remission of sins and restores the image of God in us by His efficacy.

Here are 10 bullets with sub-bullets summarizing the text:

â€¢ ••Paul's Response to Objections••
	• Paul responds to objections that the promise made to Abraham might be disannulled
	• He argues that the law could not disannul the promise

â€¢ ••First Reason: Time••
	• The promise was made to Abraham 430 years before the law was given
	• Paul argues that the law was not given to disannul the promise

â€¢ ••Objection: Abraham's Seed••
	• It may be objected that Abraham's seed was only in a strange land for 400 years
	• Paul responds that Moses speaks of the time from the beginning of Abraham's seed to the giving of the law, not the time between the promise and the law

â€¢ ••Objection: Israelites in Egypt••
	• It may be objected that the Israelites were in Egypt for 430 years
	• Paul responds that Moses' statement refers to the time of the Israelites' dwelling as pilgrims, not just their time in Egypt

â€¢ ••Second Reason: Inheritance••
	• If the law abolishes the promise, then the inheritance must come by the law
	• But this is impossible, because the inheritance comes by the promise, not the law

â€¢ ••Opposition between Law and Promise••
	• The law promises life on condition of obedience
	• The promise (gospel) offers life freely without condition, requiring only faith as a means of receiving it

â€¢ ••Faith as a Gift••
	• Faith is not a work done by us, but a free gift of God
	• It is an instrument to receive things promised, not a condition for receiving them

â€¢ ••Difference between Law and Gospel••
	• The law promises life on condition of obedience
	• The gospel (promise) offers life freely without condition, requiring only faith as a means of receiving it

â€¢ ••Justification of a Sinner••
	• If life comes by the law, it does not come by the promise
	• If those who are under the law are heirs, the promise is of no effect

â€¢ ••Importance of Understanding the Law and Promise••
	• The difference between the law and the promise is crucial for understanding true religion
	• Ignorance of this point has led to the decay of religion in the Church of Rome.

â€¢ ••Scriptural Language••
	• In Scripture, the work of a principal agent is often attributed to an instrument or minister
	• Examples include saints judging the world and angels sounding the last trumpet

â€¢ ••The Mediator••
	• The mediator is not Christ, but Moses, who delivered the law to the Israelites
	• Moses is a mediator only in the sense of relating and reporting the law from God

â€¢ ••The Nature of a Mediator••
	• A mediator is of two or more parties, and is often at variance with one of them
	• God is one and the same, without change or disagreement

â€¢ ••The Law and Transgressions••
	• The law is given to reveal transgressions and guiltiness
	• The law shows that man is at variance with God

â€¢ ••Examining Ourselves••
	• We must examine our hearts and lives by the law of God
	• Four rules must be observed when examining ourselves by the law

â€¢ ••Rules for Examining Ourselves••
	• Rule 1: All sins of the same kind are forbidden under a single commandment
	• Rule 2: Negative commandments include affirmative obligations
	• Rule 3: Every commandment must be understood with a curse annexed
	• Rule 4: We must especially examine ourselves by the first and last commandments

â€¢ ••The First and Last Commandments••
	• The first commandment forbids the first motions of our hearts against God
	• The last commandment forbids the first motions of our hearts against our neighbor

â€¢ ••The Importance of Self-Examination••

â€¢ ••The Trinity and Mediation••

	• John says that if we sin, we have an advocate with the Father.

â€¢ ••Christ's Mediation••
	• Christ's mediation is a voluntary dispensation, and as God-man, He is mediator to Himself as the Son of God.
	• Christ's mediation is essential for our salvation and spiritual growth.

• ••The Relationship Between Life and Justice••

	• This is because they do not have a change of heart and life.

	• According to Paul, Christ quickens those whom He justifies.
	• If Christ has sanctified and renewed your heart, then you are justified.
• ••The Meaning of the Scripture••
	• The text explains that the Scripture refers to the written law in the Old Testament.
	• The law is compared to a judge or sergeant, and sin is compared to a prison.
• ••The Condemnation of All Men••
	• The text argues that the law concludes all men under sin.
	• This means that all men are shut up under sin, with no way to escape.
• ••The Promise of Remission of Sin••
	• The text explains that the promise refers to remission of sin and life everlasting.
	• This promise is given to believers through the faith of Christ.
• ••The Sovereignty of God••
	• The text argues that God has an absolute sovereignty and lordship over all His creatures.
	• We may not dispute the case with God, as He may do with His own what He will.
• ••The Unsearchable Ways of God••
	• The text explains that the ways and judgments of God are unsearchable.
	• We should not try to understand the reasons behind God's actions, as they are beyond our comprehension.

â€¢ ••Our Spiritual Bondage••

â€¢ ••The Fall of Adam••

â€¢ ••The Call to Salvation••

â€¢ ••The Invitation to Believe••

	• The law was given to keep people from sin and to prevent open offenses
	• It serves to restrain and prevent sin, even if it cannot be kept perfectly

â€¢ ••The Use of the Law••

â€¢ ••The Distinction between the Church and the World••

â€¢ ••The Freedom of Will••
	• The laws of God are not in vain, even if they cannot be kept perfectly
	• They serve to restrain and prevent sin, and to keep people in order

â€¢ ••The Unity of Faith••

	• We should be protected by the wholesome precepts and counsels of God.

â€¢ ••The Law as a Schoolmaster••

â€¢ ••The Manner and Way of Salvation••
	• The law prepares us for salvation by humbling us and showing us our need for Christ.

â€¢ ••The School of God••
	• There are two forms in the school of God: the law and the gospel

	• The moral law is unchangeable and admits no abrogation

â€¢ ••The Ceremonial Law••
	• The ceremonial law prescribed rites and gestures
	• The ceremonial law was canceled by Christ's death

â€¢ ••The Judicial Law••

â€¢ ••The Resurrection of Christ••
